# Flump copy audit

2026-11-14, against `main` at `79b9bc9d`. Scored against [COPY-GUIDE.md](./COPY-GUIDE.md) version 1.0. Line numbers will drift as page PRs land: search for the "Current" text if a line has moved.

## How to use this document

- **Page sessions (design plan PRs 4 to 12):** find your PR's section, apply the rows in its tables while you are in those files, then run `bun scripts/check-copy.ts --write-baseline` and commit the baseline. A row whose "Proposed" says "removed by plan" or "Remove" needs no replacement text. If a row's string no longer exists because your redesign dropped it, skip it.
- **Shared surfaces A and B** are owned by no page PR. The copy session applies those rows itself in a follow-up PR.
- **PR 12 and the marketing files in PR 11:** the marketing pass session is fixing those files in its own PR. The rows here are for checking its replacements against the guide after it merges, not for a second rewrite.
- `{…}` in a string marks an interpolated value. Keep it where it is.
- Nothing in a "Needs the owner" list is to be changed by an agent. Those are fixed or regulated wording (guide 7.3), claims that need evidence (E7), or product decisions.

## Method

1. `bun scripts/check-copy.ts --inventory` extracted every user-facing string from `apps/web/app`, `apps/marketing/src`, `packages/ui/src`, `packages/mcp/src`, `packages/finance-engine/src` and the locale file: about 4,500 strings, grouped by the design plan's PR ownership (`scripts/lib/copy-owners.ts`).
2. One reviewer per group read its strings in context, scored each page and proposed replacements.
3. A separate compliance check read every proposed string against the advice boundary policy (R1 to R8), the fixed-wording list and E7. It failed 66 of 1,341 proposals; each failed row was corrected and is marked "compliance check" in its Rule column.

**Scoring.** Each page starts at 10. It loses 1 for each of these it breaks at all, and 2 where the breach is regulated wording (R1 to R8, E7) or judgement of the reader (guide 2.3): plain words and glossary; advice-shaped wording; judgement; estimates and numbers; case and punctuation; buttons, errors and empty states; consistency. The scale is strict on purpose: one advice-shaped sentence and one judging badge take a page to 6 however good the rest is. A 1 does not mean every string is wrong.

## What goes wrong most often

1. **Finance words where everyday ones exist** (guide 6): "Assets", "Liabilities", "Active liability accounts", "Discretionary allowance", "Liquid cash", "Monthly surplus", "Qualifying gross", "Weighted by balance (AER)", "amortisation", "widgets", "baseline", "snapshot". This is the largest group and the easiest to fix.
2. **Advice-shaped sentences that survived the E29 sweep** (R2, R8): "Available to save, invest or overpay", "Always keep an emergency fund of liquid savings before making illiquid mortgage overpayments", "Salary sacrifice (saves NI)", "priority bills rank above credit cards", overpay "before renewal … may open up lower rates".
3. **Judging or alarming an ordinary situation** (guide 2.3): danger badges for "high APR", a danger-styled notice when a budget is short, countdown badges to 5 April, milestone tiles "not reached", a health score and grade sent to AI assistants in the context pack.
4. **The £100,000 threshold as a banner or a product name** (R8, plan P7.5): "tax trap" in tool names, page titles, the locale file and a pension-page warning banner.
5. **Claims the tool on the page does not back** (E7, guide 7.4): "syncs your live balance and monitors fixed-rate renewal dates automatically", an overdraft "payoff" feature, student-loan "salary growth", a Section 24 pension input, "Live" badges on ten banks.
6. **Estimates stated as facts, and facts labelled loosely** (guide 4.4): computed dates and tax figures without "Estimate"; "Projection, not a forecast"; fixed example figures (£1,200, 39.9%) shown whatever the person entered.
7. **Notices written by hand** (R6, guide 5.7): at least eight second disclaimers beside or instead of the canonical ones, and "Unverified estimate" in lower case, in brackets, extended, doubled or missing.
8. **Mechanics** (guide 5): Title Case and ampersands in the AI connections card, bank dialogs and marketing navigation; two exclamation marks; "Something went wrong" in five places; "Reset" used for a permanent delete.

## Needs the owner

Items 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 9 and 11 below, and the bank and "free" parts of item 6, were settled by the rulings above. They are kept so the owner can see what was decided.

Nothing below was changed or proposed for change. Each group's own "Needs the owner" list has the file and line. The items that matter most:

1. **Debt charities on the budget page.** A notice shown only when a budget is short names StepChange, National Debtline and Citizens Advice with phone numbers. The policy allows one named service (MoneyHelper), shown to everyone and never triggered by a figure (R3, R4). `docs/compliance/consumer-duty-principles.md` still lists those three links as "in place". Decide: remove, or replace with the MoneyHelper line shown to every user.
2. **"Unverified estimate" is inconsistent.** It appears in lower case and brackets inside badges and headings, in a longer form with an instruction (`TakeHomeDeltaPanel/copy.ts:120`), twice on the taper tool page, and not at all on several tax figures (pension tax relief, the tax headline, the Section 24 calculator body, the AI context pack). One ruling on form and position is needed (R6).
3. **Hand-written disclaimers** sit beside or instead of the canonical notices in at least eight places ("Guidance, not advice.", "Estimates for guidance, not advice.", "illustrations … not advice", `TAX_DISCLAIMER`, `TAX_NOTICE`, a note in `getTaxYearPosition`, extra children inside `ProjectionNotice`). `TAX_DISCLAIMER` also states regulatory status. Decide which stay.
4. **Where the £100,000 line lives.** Plan P7.5 says only inside the "next £1,000" tool; the policy's R5 example puts it, with a "Model this contribution" button, on the pension page.
5. **Estimates in the hero style.** Plan decision D2 is still open and four pages already do it (debt-free date, take-home, tax estimate, onboarding summary).
6. **Claims that need evidence in the E7 register:** "Flump has no feature to make payments", "read-only", "cannot transfer funds", "Live" on ten banks against "preview for Monzo and Starling only", "Verified" email, "Official MCP SDK v1.0", generic bank labels "by default", MCP rate limits, "nothing you enter is saved" (the calculators write to local storage on sign-up), "free" in headings and buttons, "UK standard is about 39.9%", "up to 10% a year" overpayment allowance.
7. **Childcare lines.** Several surfaces say funded hours and Tax-Free Childcare stop above £100,000. Guide 7.5 (owner decision of 2026-11-13) says benefits are signposted only. Confirm whether childcare support counts.
8. **Provider names as examples** in two add-account dialogs (Nest, Vanguard, Marcus). Strict R3 removes them.
9. **The AI context pack** sends a health score, a grade ("CRITICAL" to "EXCELLENT") and a stage to third-party models, with no notice, and states defaults (3.5% AER) as the person's facts. This needs a code change, not copy (2.3, R4, R7).
10. **MCP tool names and result keys** break the glossary (`get_financial_snapshot`, `emergencyRunwayMonths`, `liabilitiesGbp`, `taxTrap`, `contributionRequiredToEscapeGbp`). Renaming is an API change.
11. **Onboarding has no housing option for living with family** or paying no rent. The owner rule gives that equal weight; adding it changes the data model.
12. **Risk warnings in a regulated style** on two tool guides ("capital is at risk", "values can fall as well as rise"), and the Section 24 tax-rule list that is still a pending ruling in the policy log.
13. **"Reset all financial data"** is a permanent delete. Renaming it changes the typed confirmation word.
14. **`MCP_NOTICE` ends "Unverified."** on every result, including lists of the person's own saved figures.
15. **Pre-filled budget amounts** (£1,200 rent and so on) act as anchors (guide 4.4, rule 10).
16. **Marketing** (PR 12): all of it waits for owner sign-off under plan decision D15. Pricing sentences contradict each other ("not announced" against "free beta"), bank connection is "planned" on one page and "coming soon" on six, and the vulnerability-report address is an unmonitored placeholder.

Not audited: `apps/mobile-companion`, the backend's API error bodies, legal pages (none exist yet) and emails (the product sends none).

## PR 4. Dashboard and sandbox

### Dashboard `/app`: 2/10

Problems:

- Never-use words on almost every card: "widget", "module", "snapshot", "baseline", "assets", "liabilities", "liability", "monthly surplus", "discretionary allowance", "liquid cash", "gross", "net pay", "refine", "drill down", "amortisation" (guide 6.1, 6.2, 3.2).
- Advice-shaped wording: "Available to save, invest or overpay" lists what to do with money under a saved figure (R2), and three setup checklist steps are financial activities rather than app housekeeping (R4, guide 5.12).
- Judgement: a red "high APR" badge grades a debt (guide 2.3, 4.2, R3), "Monthly deficit" is shown in the danger tone (2.3 item 8), and "Widget unlocked on the dashboard" is hype (2.2).
- Estimates are not called estimates: "forecast" as a label, "Projected debt-free date", an unlabelled debt-free date, and "An estimate, not a promise" as a hand-written disclaimer (guide 4.4 items 1 and 4, 5.7). "10–30 year" uses an en dash in prose (4.3).
- Buttons, errors and empty states: "No widgets showing" (5.5), an empty state with two buttons (5.5), an error with no data state (5.6), and "Not calculated" where the status word is "Not set" (5.10).
- One concept, several names: "debt-free date", "payoff date" and "debt payoff"; "monthly surplus" and "safe to spend allowance"; "Self Assessment liability" and "tax due" (guide 6.1).
- Removed by plan (no rewrite needed): "Welcome back{…}" h1 and the "Your live financial snapshot across your active modules." subtitle (`HomeDashboard.tsx:247,250`); the net worth KPI card and its "Add an account" not-set link (`DashboardKpiStrip.tsx:68-71`); the "Net worth" headline in `AccountsWidget.tsx:84`; the "Unsecured debt" band above the hero (`HomeDashboard.tsx:255`), whose content moves into the debt section card.

### Sandbox (`/sandbox`, `/app/sandbox`, SandboxBar, SampleDataNote): 1/10

Problems:

- Claims the visitor's session cannot back: "Live Demo Sandbox", "your own bank feeds and HMRC data" and "Save my numbers" on a page of sample figures (E7, guide 6.2, 7.4).
- Advice-shaped and celebratory template copy: "Crossover Milestone Reached", "You are redirecting £515/month ... into a tax-free ISA portfolio", "Explore ISA Scenarios", "Interest Saved" (R2, R4, R8, guide 2.4, 7.2).
- Judgement badges: red "High APR", green "Zero Liabilities", "Mandatory" (guide 2.3, 4.2, R3).
- Estimates and numbers: "7% Projected Return" and "10-Year Value" with no "estimate" or "assumes", "Year 1.5", "£315/mo" in a card (guide 4.1, 4.3, 4.4).
- Title Case and ampersands throughout `SandboxTemplates`, plus "demo" for sample data and "amortisation" (guide 5.1, 5.4, 6.1, 6.2).
- The sample-data sentence appears three times on one page (5.7, plan P4.4). Removed by plan: the `SampleDataNote` line (`sandbox.tsx:121-123`), the subtitle repeat (`HomeDashboard.tsx:246`) and the top banner.
- `SandboxTemplates` is imported only by its own story and test. Check whether PR 4 deletes it; if so, its rows below fall away.

### Needs the owner

See "Rulings of 2026-11-14" near the top of this document: several items below are now decided.

- `apps/web/app/features/dashboard/components/TaxWidget/TaxWidget.tsx:91`: the tax due figure carries "Estimate only", not the fixed "Unverified estimate" (R6, guide 4.4 item 8). The code comment says the figure is null until verified, so this may be right. The owner should confirm which label a verified tax estimate carries, and whether "Calculated once tax estimates are verified" is acceptable beside a hidden figure.
- `apps/web/app/features/dashboard/components/HomeDashboard/HomeDashboard.tsx:229`: `ProjectionNotice` sits above the h1 on the dashboard and the sandbox. The plan moves it to the foot of the card grid. No wording change is proposed; the placement of fixed wording needs the owner's nod.
- `apps/web/app/features/signed-out/components/SandboxBar/SandboxBar.tsx:57-66`: "your own bank feeds and HMRC data" and "Save my numbers" are feature claims on a signed-out page. The handler saves only which sample household was chosen, and nothing in this group shows an HMRC connection. Proposed "Ready to use your own figures?" and "Sign up" need an E7 register entry or owner approval.
- Guide against plan: the plan's dashboard KPI is "left over this month" and its h1 is "Your money"; the guide's glossary has "left over each month" and "your picture" or "your figures" for the dashboard. One form needs choosing before PR 4 lands.
- `apps/web/app/routes/sandbox.tsx:42`: "Paying a mortgage and letting a property" is neutral, but the three sample households are a renter, someone paying off debts, and a landlord on £115,000. The owner may want to check the set reads as even-handed (guide 2.3 item 1).

## PR 5. Accounts, history and budget

### Accounts (list, account detail, bank connections): 6/10

Problems:

- Banned glossary words on the summary and the bank block: "assets", "liabilities", "Weighted by balance (AER)", "Synced", "credentials", "balance feeds" (guide 6.1, 6.2, P7.1).
- One concept, several words: "Amount owed" on the detail page against "Total liabilities" on the list; "Synced" against "Connected" for a bank (6.1, 5.9).
- Errors and statuses that do not say what happened or what to do: "Account not found.", a bare "Error" badge, "Bank connections via OAuth are not available." (5.6, 3.2).
- The change figure "{…}% on last month" carries its direction only in an arrow and colour, and does not say what the percentage is of (3.3, 4.2).
- The disconnect dialog says "removed" for data that is deleted, and its confirm button has no object (5.9, 5.3).
- Removed by plan (no rewrite needed): the "Where you stand" strip ("Total assets", "Total liabilities", "Cash and savings", "Average interest", "Weighted by balance (AER)", "Nothing owed" and their sub-lines) and the "Where your assets sit" bars in `apps/web/app/features/accounts/components/AccountsSummary/AccountsSummary.tsx:47-113`; the "ISA allowance" card at `apps/web/app/containers/accounts/accountsContainer/AccountsContainer.tsx:142`; the "Synced bank connections" block while bank connect is unavailable (D8). The bank rows below apply when that block returns.

### Net worth history: 5/10

Problems:

- A computed future figure is labelled "Projection" and described as "Projection, not a forecast", where the one approved word is "estimate" (4.4.1, 6.1, R8).
- A hand-written disclaimer sits inside `ProjectionNotice` on the first-month view, against "never write a second disclaimer by hand" (5.7, R6).
- "Budget surplus" is used twice; the glossary term is "left over each month" (6.1).
- The save error uses a negative contraction and "Please try again" (5.6, 2.1).
- Three words for one thing in the chart and its notice: "projection", "forecast" and "dashed line"; "Basis" as a column header is not a kitchen-table word (3.2, 6.1).

### Budget (including the setup wizard): 1/10

Problems:

- A notice shown only when spending is more than income carries a danger icon and badge, the heading "Your budget spends more than you bring in", a line ranking which bills come first, and three named debt services (guide 2.3 point 8, 7.5, R2, R3, R4).
- Glossary breaches: "Discretionary" (badge, toggle, checkbox, months-of-cover rows), "Monthly shortfall", "Spending exceeds income", "Priority bills" against "essential spending" (6.1, 6.2).
- Pre-filled amounts are called "suggested defaults" and a "Standard UK template" with set figures, where a default is neutral and an example is labelled "Example" (4.4.10); safe to spend is also converted to "a day" (4.1); "Annual" for "a year" (3.4).
- Feature names written as proper nouns: "Savings Hub", "Savings forecast", "the Budget page" (5.1); "forecast" used as a label (4.4.1).
- Buttons and errors: "Create budget" shows when a budget exists (5.3, P7.4); all five save errors use "couldn't" and "Please try again" with no state line (5.6); delete dialogs say "removes" and "can't" (5.9, 2.1).
- One thing, three names: "entry", "item" and "budget lines"; "Add entry" beside "Add an entry"; "Per month" beside "a month" (6, 5.3).
- Removed by plan (no rewrite needed): the "Fixed and flexible" cards ("Priority bills", "Flexible spending" and their notes, `BudgetOverview.tsx:285-303`); the donut and its legend ("Where it goes", "Expense breakdown by category", "Expenses", `ExpenseBreakdown.tsx:127-180`); the pill nav (`BudgetNav.tsx`, "Budget sections", "Overview"); the delta labels "Spending exceeds income" and "Left over" once "Left over each month" is the hero.

### Needs the owner

See "Rulings of 2026-11-14" near the top of this document: several items below are now decided.

- `apps/web/app/features/budget/components/BudgetOverview/BudgetOverview.tsx:236-281`: the notice shown only when the budget is short each month names StepChange, National Debtline and Citizens Advice with phone numbers. R3 allows MoneyHelper only, and R3, R4 and guide 7.5 say debt help is never triggered by a figure. The plan's budget section does not list this block as removed. The audit proposes removing it; the owner decides whether the fixed MoneyHelper line may also appear on the budget page for every user.
- `apps/web/app/features/snapshot/components/SnapshotHistoryPage/SnapshotHistoryPage.tsx:218-222`: `ProjectionNotice` is given hand-written children ("Projection, not a forecast: ..."). Guide 5.7 and R6 say one notice from the canonical constant, never reworded. Owner to confirm whether the component may carry extra text, or whether the assumption moves to a separate line as proposed.
- `apps/web/app/features/budget/components/BudgetSetupWizard/BudgetSetupWizard.tsx:455` and `apps/web/app/containers/budget/BudgetContainer.tsx:338`: the wizard and the empty state pre-fill fixed amounts (£1,200 rent or mortgage and so on). Guide 4.4.10 says defaults are neutral (the person's own figure, or empty). Relabelling as "Example" is the copy fix; whether pre-filled amounts stay at all is a product call.

## PR 6. Debt and mortgage

### Debt: 3/10

Problems:

- Glossary breaches throughout: "liability", "creditor", "surplus", "clear/cleared", "window", "revert APR", "balloon or GFV", "gross", "outlay" (guide 6.1, 6.2, 3.2).
- Alarm and judgement on ordinary situations: a danger banner titled "Promotional window alert", "diagnosis", "Staying in a £X overdraft", "do not clear the debt yet" (guide 2.3 point 8).
- Computed future figures are not called estimates: "Debt-free by", "Interest to pay", "Not cleared within the projection"; a finish date that cannot be worked out shows "Not set", which reads as missing input (guide 4.4, 5.10).
- One concept, several names: "Debt-free by", "Finish date", "debt-free date"; "Your debts", "Active liability accounts", "Liability accounts"; "minimums", "minimum payments", "minimum repayments" (guide 6.1).
- Errors and empty states: "Something went wrong. Please try again.", "Unknown action.", "No debts yet", "No active debts recorded." with a full stop on a heading (guide 5.5, 5.6, 5.4).
- R1 holds: both payoff orders carry the same fields and verbs. Two small slips: the two hints are not parallel, and "Match surplus" is a pre-fill button that should say "Use" (R1, R5 ruling). An unbacked figure sits in a hint: "UK standard is about 39.9%" (guide 4.4 point 10, 7.4).
- Removed by plan (D5), no rewrite: every string in `apps/web/app/features/debt/components/DebtToWealthBridgeCard/DebtToWealthBridgeCard.tsx` (it also breaks 6.1 "wealth", 4.4 "Projection, not a forecast" and R3 by naming ISA types as destinations). Also removed by plan: the tab bar in the empty state and the "Not set" APR cell, replaced by one prompt.

### Mortgage: 1/10

Problems:

- Advice-shaped wording in two results: "Always keep an emergency fund of liquid savings before making illiquid mortgage overpayments" and "overpay {…}/mo before renewal … this may open up lower rates" (R2). The overpay or save note also gives only the points in favour of overpaying, and its two rates are styled danger and positive (R1).
- Minimisers and hype: "a little extra each month" (twice), "take your time", "Time shaved off", "Unlocked", "Locked", "/mo to unlock", "Lowest rate tier active" (a claim about lender pricing we cannot back) (guide 2.2, 2.3 point 7, 7.4).
- Jargon and banned terms: "simulator", "Simulate rate shock", "remortgage shock", "payment shock", "bullet", "principal", "horizon", "parameters", "outflow", "liquid", "gross", "net cash flow" (guide 6.1, 6.2, 3.2).
- Estimates: "you will pay" twice; "/mo" and "per month" in prose; three figures in one sentence; a hand-written "Projection, not a forecast" beside the page notice (guide 4.4 points 1 and 7, 4.1, 3.1, 5.7).
- One concept, several names: "tier", "bracket", "band", "threshold"; "fix", "fixed deal", "fixed-rate deal", "fixed rate"; "Saved to your mortgage" and "Stored on your mortgage account"; "Overpay vs save", "Overpay or save" (guide 6.1).
- Case, punctuation and states: "vs.", "ISA / savings", "principal +", Title Case chart series, "Mortgage account missing", "Please try again" (guide 5.1, 5.4, 5.6, 5.10).

### Housing cost card: 9/10

Problems:

- The save error does not say what state the data is in and uses "Please try again" (guide 5.6).
- Renting and paying a mortgage get the same heading, the same share line and the same styling, so the owner's rule holds (guide 2.3 point 1). One thing to check: when housing status is not set, the card asks for "your monthly rent", which assumes renting.

| File and line                                                                      | Current                                           | Proposed                                                            | Rule                                                                                                                                               |
| ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `apps/web/app/features/housing/components/HousingCostCard/HousingCostCard.tsx:41`  | Not known yet. Add your monthly rent to see this. | Not known yet. Add what you pay for housing each month to see this. | 2.3 point 1 (check: this text also shows when housing status is not set or is "own outright"; keep the current wording if it only reaches renters) |
| `apps/web/app/features/housing/components/HousingCostCard/HousingCostCard.tsx:109` | Rent saved.                                       | Rent saved                                                          | 5.10, 5.4                                                                                                                                          |

Try again.        | 5.6                                                                                                                                                |

### Needs the owner

See "Rulings of 2026-11-14" near the top of this document: several items below are now decided.

- `apps/web/app/features/debt/components/DebtHelpLink/DebtHelpLink.tsx:17`: the region's accessible name is "Debt advice", but the fixed line (`:18`) says "debt help". A screen reader hears "advice" for a signpost the policy calls "not a recommendation". The guide also disagrees with itself: 5.3 gives "MoneyHelper debt advice (opens in a new tab)" as the model link, while 7.3 fixes "Free, impartial debt help is available from MoneyHelper". No rewrite proposed; the owner picks one word.
- `apps/web/app/features/debt/components/DebtHelpLink/DebtHelpLink.tsx:18`: "Free" in the fixed line is a claim about a third party's service (guide 7.4). It needs an entry in the E7 claim register if it has none.
- `apps/web/app/features/mortgage/components/BtlOverview/BtlOverview.tsx:106`: a Section 24 tax rule sits under the user's own buy to let cash flow figure. The rulings log says a rule list directly under a computed tax figure is removed. This is a cash figure, not a tax figure, so a plainer wording is proposed above; the owner may prefer to remove the line.
- Plan decision D2 (may the hero hold an estimate): the debt strip currently puts "Debt-free by" in the hero tone (`DebtSummary.tsx:52`). The proposed label "Debt-free date (estimate)" works for either answer, but the hero tone itself waits on D2.
- `apps/web/app/features/debt/components/UkDebtInstrumentsCard/UkDebtInstrumentsCard.tsx:281` ("UK standard is about 39.9%") and the "up to 10% a year" overpayment line (`OverpaymentSimulator.tsx:51`, `OverpayVsSaveCompare.tsx:57`): market claims with no evidence in the repo that I could find. Rewrites are proposed above; the figures need an approver or removal.

## PR 7. Pension and savings

### Pension: 1/10

Problems:

- The £100,000 to £125,140 band is a warning-styled banner with a heading, a warning icon and a warning badge. Guide 7.2 and P7.5 allow one factual line only, never a banner or headline. It also writes "£100k", uses an en dash in prose and says "marginal rate" (R8, 7.2, 2.3 point 8, 4.1, 4.3).
- Benefit claims and steers on money choices: "Salary sacrifice (saves NI)", provider names in a placeholder, "keep growing" as a promise (R2, R3, R8, 4.4 point 7).
- Judgement: the PLSA tier labels grade the person's income ("Below the PLSA minimum") and the annual allowance line uses the warning style for an ordinary figure (2.3 points 5 and 8, 5.7). The plan removes the PLSA bar (D6).
- Estimates: figures are labelled "Projected" or "Forecast" rather than "Estimate"; "projection, not a forecast" sits under a tab called "Forecast"; the estimate note is one 40-word sentence with 4 figures; the 5% growth default is called a "placeholder" (4.4 points 1, 4 and 10, 3.1).
- Glossary and plain words: "qualifying earnings", "weighted by value", "drawdown", "net pay", "carry-forward", "levers", bare "take-home" (6.1, 6.2, 3.2).
- Same concept, several names: "Expected return", "Return", "Assumed growth", "Growth a year"; "Regular payment", "Paid in", "Contributions". Also an ampersand in the page title, semicolons joining clauses and "can't" in a destructive confirmation (5.4, 5.9, 2.1).

Removed by plan (no rewrite): quick growth-rate chips (`ForecasterControls.tsx:102`, `:111`); PLSA labels, bar and scale (`ForecasterResults.tsx:31-34`, `:87-97`, and the tier in `:64`); two-figure captions (`ForecasterResults.tsx:55-56`, `PensionSummary.tsx:75`, `:94-96`); the "Employer and growth ..., of which ..." sentence (`ForecasterResults.tsx:76-78`).

### Savings: 1/10

Problems:

- The allowance section pushes a deadline: a warning badge with a clock ("{…} days until 5 April") beside "{…} left to use this tax year". That reads as "use your allowance before the deadline" (R2, 7.2 row 3, 2.3 point 8, 5.7). Provider names appear in a placeholder (R3).
- Judgement: fixed milestone tiles up to £1,000,000 with "Not reached in 60 years" and "You are already past this", and the heading "Where you stand", grade the person's savings (2.3 points 1 and 6). The plan removes the milestones (D4).
- Estimates: "Forecast", "Projected" and "Projection" are used as labels on figures; "real returns will differ" uses "will"; the goal field has a "40,000" placeholder that anchors (4.4 points 1, 7 and 10).
- Numbers: the deposit result gives a duration over 24 months in months first; the chart summary carries 4 figures; "Interest rate" has no type on the slider (4.3, 5.13, 4.2).
- Glossary and case: "Weighted by balance (AER)", "cash ISA" and "stocks and shares ISA" in lower case, an ampersand in the page title (6.1, 6.2, 5.1, 5.4).
- Same concept, several names: "Saved each month", "Each month", "monthly contribution", "Monthly saving", "You add", "Contributions only"; "Interest rate (AER)", "AER", "Average interest rate". "can't" in the destructive confirmation (5.9, 2.1).

Removed by plan (no rewrite): milestone tiles and their status text (`SavingsForecasterCard.tsx:397-400`, `:428-444`); the three result cards (`SavingsForecasterCard.tsx:374-386`), replaced by one outcome "About {…} in {…} years"; the "Interest in a year" and "Projected in 10 years" KPIs (`SavingsSummaryCards.tsx:85-91`), since the strip keeps saved each month, average rate and ISA allowance used.

### Needs the owner

See "Rulings of 2026-11-14" near the top of this document: several items below are now decided.

- `apps/web/app/containers/pension/PensionContainer.tsx:336-383`: guide 7.2 and P7.5 say the £100,000 line lives only inside the "next £1,000" tool, but the policy's R5 example places that sentence and the "Model this contribution" button on the pension page. The owner needs to say which holds. Until then the rewrite above keeps it on the page as one plain line.
- `apps/web/app/containers/pension/PensionContainer.tsx:343`: the fixed wording "Unverified estimate" appears in lower case, in brackets, inside a heading. No replacement is proposed; it needs a ruling on form and position once the heading goes.
- `apps/web/app/features/pension/components/PensionSummary/PensionSummary.tsx:90-99` and `PensionContainer.tsx:304`: the cost after tax relief and the tax relief amount are tax outputs, and this surface shows no "Unverified estimate" unless income is in the band (R6, guide 4.4 point 8). The owner needs to confirm whether the label is needed here.
- `apps/web/app/features/pension/components/AddPensionAccountDialog/AddPensionAccountDialog.tsx:108` and `apps/web/app/features/savings/components/AddSavingsAccountDialog/AddSavingsAccountDialog.tsx:69`: provider names (Nest, Vanguard, Marcus) in name-field examples. The rewrites remove them under a strict reading of R3; the owner may rule that an example of a name is not a suggestion.
- `apps/web/app/utils/taxTrapCopy.ts:28` (outside this group's files, feeds the pension badge): "{…} income tax, {…} including NI" writes "income tax" in lower case, and the file and symbol names use "tax trap" (guide 2.3 point 9 on internal names).

## PR 8. Take-home and Self Assessment

### Take-home pay: 4/10

Problems:

- "Gross" in nine places ("Gross salary", "Gross pay", "Gross income", "Qualifying gross", "(gross)") where the glossary has "salary before tax" or "pay before tax", and "Qualifying gross" is on the never-use list (6.1, 6.2, plan PR 8 copy).
- "Salary sacrifice (saves NI)" puts a benefit claim on a pension choice, and "Employer reinvests their 15% NI saving" does the same in a hint (R8 "save tax" family, 5.8 hints are facts, 6.3).
- Terms a PAYE employee would not know appear with no plain definition: "relief at source", "Pass on employer NI", "Effective tax rate" (which here also counts pension and student loan, so the label is not true to the figure) (3.2, 5.8, 4.2).
- Per-day and per-week conversions of pay and pence on the headline figure (4.1; both removed by plan D7 and P8).
- The same thing has several names: "Pension" and "Pension contribution"; "Student loan" and "Student loan repayment"; "Where your salary goes" against the glossary's "where your pay goes"; "Where you stand" used as a heading on both pages for different content (6.1, 5.1).
- The no-salary state is a bare sentence with no heading or button, and the cross-tool button is 6 words where the approved form is "Model this in savings" (5.5, 5.3, 5.1 table).
- Removed by plan (no rewrite): the weekly and daily strip (`TakeHomeCalculator.tsx:256-262`), the donut and its "Salary allocation" title (`TakeHomeBreakdown.tsx:76-80`), the bottom disclaimer line, any £100,000 banner.

### Self Assessment (tax): 1/10

Problems:

- The £100,000 threshold is a card with its own headline ("Personal Allowance taper, £100,000–£125,140", "Near the £100,000 income limit…") and a "Personal Allowance reduced by" tile. The policy allows one factual line inside the "next £1,000" tool, shown only in the band (R8, 7.2, plan P7.5). The fixed "Unverified estimate" wording is also reshaped inside a badge there, and is not on the headline bill in these files (R6, 4.4.8).
- Tax terms a PAYE employee would not know, none with a hint: "payments on account", "balancing bill", "Class 4 National Insurance", "Section 24 finance cost credit", "benefits in kind", "adjusted net income", "trading allowance", "cash-flow reserve", "retainers" (3.2, 5.8).
- An ordinary deadline carries a warning badge ("{…} days left"), and the set-aside figure is styled as a positive (2.3 point 8, 5.7).
- Number and date slips: "/ mo" and "/mo" in prose positions, "31 Mar", an en dash range and a "+" in running text, "self-assessment" in lower case, "e.g." (4.1, 4.3, 6.1, 3.2).
- Destructive dialogs use "can't", mix "removes" with "Delete", do not name the record, and the confirm button says "Delete year" for a delete of records. Errors give no next step ("We couldn't read those records.", "Not found") (5.9, 2.1, 5.6).
- One concept, several names: "Cost", "Costs" and "Expense", "Expenses"; "record" and "item"; "tax planner" for this page; "Add a record", "Add record" and "Add"; "Estimated Self Assessment due" and "Self Assessment tax and NI due"; "Your inputs" where the glossary has "your figures" (6.1, 5.3).
- Removed by plan (no rewrite): `TaxTrapAlertBanner` as a banner; the "next £1,000" zone on this page (`TaxContainer.tsx:428-436`, link only); "Quick add", "Hide quick add" and both "Copy from" buttons (`TaxIncomeSectionCard.tsx:367-378`, `470`; folded into one "Add income" menu with "Copy from last year"); the header-row disclaimer; "0 records" badges.

### Needs the owner

See "Rulings of 2026-11-14" near the top of this document: several items below are now decided.

- `apps/web/app/features/tax/components/TaxTrapAlertBanner/TaxTrapAlertBanner.tsx:83-84`: the fixed "Unverified estimate" wording is lower-cased and bracketed inside a rate badge ("{…} marginal rate including NI (unverified estimate)"). Not rewritten here. The plan removes the banner; if the rate survives in the "next £1,000" tool, the owner needs to say how the fixed wording sits beside it.
- `apps/web/app/features/tax/components/TaxHeadline/TaxHeadline.tsx:29` and `apps/web/app/components/tax/TaxEstimateNote/TaxEstimateNote.tsx:27`: within this group's files the headline tax figure and the shared note say "Estimate" but never "Unverified estimate". The only carrier on the page appears to be `TaxDisclaimerButton` in the header, which the plan removes ("the header-row disclaimer"). R6 and 4.4.8 need it once per surface, and the plan puts it as a badge on the hero. The take-home page has the same gap. Owner to confirm where the one instance lives on each page.
- `apps/web/app/features/tax/components/TaxHeadline/TaxHeadline.tsx:31` and `apps/web/app/features/take-home/components/TakeHomeCalculator/TakeHomeCalculator.tsx:244`: both tax estimates use the hero tone. Guide 4.4.9 keeps the hero style for saved figures; the plan specifies a `HeroFigure` for each. This is a conflict between the guide and the plan, not a copy fix.
- `apps/web/app/features/tax/components/TaxSourcesPanel/TaxSourcesPanel.tsx:137` ("ISA interest is tax-free.") and `PaymentsOnAccountCard.tsx:71` (the £1,000 payments on account rule): flat statements of tax law beside the user's own figures. They read as facts, but the rulings log says a list of tax rules directly under a computed tax figure is removed rather than reworded (R2 ruling, 2026-11-13, pending). Owner or solicitor to say whether these single lines stay.
- `apps/web/app/features/tax/components/TaxTrapAlertBanner/TaxTrapAlertBanner.tsx:94-96`: the Tax-Free Childcare and funded hours sentence states an eligibility limit. Guide 7.5 says benefits are signpost only. Proposed for removal above; owner to confirm childcare support counts as a benefit for that rule.

## PR 9. Settings, onboarding, app chrome, loading and error states

### Settings (profile): 7/10

Problems:

- "Module" is used for a page section in a heading, a label and an error (6.1, 6.2, P7.1). The plan renames the heading to "Sections you use".
- The data tab uses "Reset" for an action that deletes everything for good. Guide 5.9 keeps "Reset" for returning to saved or default values and "Delete" for permanent removal. "Reset", "removes", "deletes" and "wipes" all name the same act (consistency).
- The destructive dialog title is not a question, the body does not say what stays, and the confirm button does not repeat the verb (5.9). The typed word `RESET` (`hooks/useUserProfile.ts:274`) would need to change with the verb.
- Success text is chatty: "You have a clean slate" (5.10, 2.4).
- `DataTab.tsx:70-73` and `SecurityTab.tsx:41-44` show a raw server `err.message` when there is one (5.6: never a raw server message).
- h1 is "Your settings"; the plan prescribes "Settings". Button "Remove" has no object (5.3, 5.13).
- Removed by plan: the three "Go to ..." buttons become text links (`ProfileTab.tsx:377,386,395`); the wording below is for the links.

### Onboarding: 4/10

Problems:

- Housing has three options, "Renting", "Mortgage" and "Own outright", under the legend "Housing status". There is no option for living with family or paying no rent, so that person must pick "Renting" and leave rent empty (2.3 rule 1). Adding the option is a product change: check with the owner. "Status" also reads as a grade; and the own-outright review row is labelled "Monthly rent".
- Glossary breaches in labels: "Monthly surplus", "Net take-home", "Annual gross salary", "Modules switched on", "demo" (6.1, 6.2, P7.1). The same field is "Salary before tax" in one control and "Annual gross salary" in the next (consistency, 5.11).
- "Typical salaries" preset buttons are anchors and a population comparison (4.4 rule 10, 2.3 rules 4 and 5). The label must read "Example".
- Numbers and marks: "/mo" in review prose rows (4.1), "e.g." in every placeholder (3.2), "Saving..." with three full stops (5.3), "Postgraduate Loan" (5.1), "+£…" on the left-over figure.
- Errors use negative contractions ("couldn't") and "Needed to finish setup." is stuck on the end of a hint, twice (2.1, 5.8, 5.11). The label "Where do you pay tax?" has the placeholder "Choose where you live", which is a different question.
- The review shows the computed monthly take-home in the hero style (`FinishSummary.tsx:158-167`, `tone="hero"`); check against 4.4 rule 9. "Skip for now" is the primary button on the bank step (plan: text link on optional steps only).
- Removed by plan: "Monthly surplus" and "Starting net worth" in `LiveSummaryBar.tsx:56,66` (one figure only); "Not applicable" rows (`FinishSummary.tsx:58`); "Modules switched on" becomes "Sections we have set up for you".

### Navigation and header: 8/10

Problems:

- Title Case in an accessible name: "Mobile Bottom Navigation" (5.1, 5.13).
- "Log out" sits beside "Sign in", "Sign up" and "signing in" everywhere else (consistency).
- "More navigation options" is long for a control name; the plan calls the item "More" (5.13).
- Removed by plan: the hamburger at 375, so "Open navigation menu" (`Header.tsx:80`) goes on mobile.

| File and line                                                         | Current                  | Proposed          | Rule                       |
| --------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------ | ----------------- | -------------------------- |
| `apps/web/app/components/navigation/AppBottomBar/AppBottomBar.tsx:54` | Mobile Bottom Navigation | Main navigation   | 5.1, 5.13                  |
| `apps/web/app/components/navigation/AppBottomBar/AppBottomBar.tsx:33` | More navigation options  | More pages        | 5.13                       |
| `apps/web/app/components/navigation/AvatarMenu/AvatarMenu.tsx:103`    | Log out                  | Sign out          | consistency with "Sign in" |
| `apps/web/app/components/navigation/AvatarMenu/AvatarMenu.tsx:76`     | Account menu             | Your account menu | 5.13                       |

### Dialogs (`components/dialogs`): 5/10

Problems:

- Claims the product cannot back (E7, 7.4, 6.2): every one of the ten banks carries a "Live" badge (`directSync: true` on all) while the copy beside it says connection is a preview for Monzo and Starling only; "coming soon" promises an unbuilt feature; "secure" is used four times as a bare adjective. The policy's own R3 table names `Invest, Stocks ISA & CFD sync` as a line to reword.
- Developer wording shown to users: "OAuth portal", "OAuth 2.0", "server OAuth client keys configured in your environment", "(.env)", "Establishing secure API link and parsing balances", "dummy data", "mock accounts" (3.2, 6.1 sample data).
- "Sync", "synced" and "credentials" (6.1, 6.2), and nine ampersands plus "Connect & Sync" and "Developer Token" in Title Case (5.4, 5.1).
- Errors: "Please try again", "couldn't", the label "Error message" above a raw server message, and a fallback that tells the user to check a `.env` file (5.6, 2.1).
- One thing, many names: "developer token", "personal access token", "access token", "bearer token", "API key"; "Connect to bank", "Connect bank account", "Link with", "Authorise via" (consistency).
- The delete-account confirmation does not say whether it can be undone (5.9).

### Error, 404 and callback pages: 8/10

Problems:

- "Something went wrong" is the document title for every non-404 error and opens the callback description (5.6, on the never list).
- The callback description is three sentences with negative contractions ("haven't") and the word "session" (5.6: at most two short sentences; 2.1; 3.2).
- The 404 title is a label, not the guide's sentence (5.6: "We cannot find that page."). The visible error body comes from `features/feedback/components/RouteErrorBoundary`, outside this group.

### Needs the owner

See "Rulings of 2026-11-14" near the top of this document: several items below are now decided.

- `apps/web/app/components/dialogs/addEditAccountsDialog/AddEditAccountsDialog.tsx:611` ("Flump has no feature to make payments.") and `apps/web/app/components/dialogs/connectBankWizard/ConnectBankWizard.tsx:447-448` ("Read-only access", "Flump cannot transfer funds or modify bank settings."): security and capability claims. Not rewritten. They need an entry in the E7 claim register with evidence that every connection method is read-only, including a user-supplied token with wider scopes.
- `apps/web/app/components/dialogs/addEditAccountsDialog/AddEditAccountsDialog.tsx:639` and `ConnectBankWizard.tsx:321`: "Live" on all ten banks contradicts "preview for Monzo and Starling only" in the same dialog. The owner needs to say which banks connect today before the badge wording is settled (E7).
- `apps/web/app/features/settings/components/SecurityTab/SecurityTab.tsx:71`: "Verified" beside the account email is a status claim. Check it is driven by the auth provider's verified flag and not shown for every email (E7).
- `apps/web/app/features/onboarding/components/HousingStep/HousingStep.tsx:7-11`: no housing option for people living with family or paying no rent. Owner rule 2.3 gives that situation equal weight; adding it is a product and data-model decision.
- `apps/web/app/features/onboarding/components/FinishSummary/FinishSummary.tsx:158-167`: the review puts an estimated take-home figure in the hero style next to `ProjectionNotice`. Guide 4.4 rule 9 keeps the hero style for saved figures; the plan keeps this figure. The two need reconciling.
- `apps/web/app/features/settings/components/DataTab`: renaming "Reset" to "Delete" changes the typed confirmation word (`RESET_FINANCIAL_DATA_CONFIRMATION`) and tests. Owner to confirm the verb, and what is kept after the deletion (sign-in, name, picture).

## PR 10. Calculator bodies (`packages/ui/src/calculators`)

Every calculator's sign-up button gets the same label, "Continue to sign up" (already used by the taper calculator), so the rows below repeat it. `MarginalRateExplorer` is in this PR's file list but its strings live in `packages/ui/src/patterns` and were not in this inventory, so it is not scored here.

### Debt snowball calculator: 3/10

Problems:

- The two methods are not shown with the same fields: "Shaves" and "interest saved" appear for avalanche only, and snowball gets a different sentence (R1, guide 4.5).
- The button says "Track live" on a signed-out page (E7, guide 6.2) and runs to 5 words with an arrow (5.3).
- "Liability", "surplus" and "strategy" are off the glossary (6.1, 6.2; plan: "Liability 1" becomes "Debt 1").
- No computed date or interest figure is called an estimate, and "will not" is used for an outcome (4.4.1, 4.4.7).
- Labels use "Min / mo" and "/mo" where the period belongs in words (4.1, 5.11).
- The same thing has three names: "Debt free", "Full payoff", "clear the debt" (6.1: "paid off", "debt-free date").

### Overdraft cost calculator: 2/10

Problems:

- "Clearing a £1,200 overdraft delivers an immediate guaranteed 39.9% tax-free return" is a recommendation with a banned outcome word (R2, R8, guide 4.4.7).
- "Why overdrafts are dangerous", "stealth annual fee" and the danger styling alarm an ordinary situation (2.3.8, 5.7).
- The callout is fixed text: it always says "£1,200", "39.9%" and "40% APR" whatever balance and rate are entered, so it shows example figures as if they were the result (7.4, 4.4.10, 2.3.4).
- "Eliminate your overdraft with Flump" is an imperative about debt and an outcome promise (R2, 6.1, 5.3).
- The rate label suggests a value to enter, "UK standard is ~39.9%" (4.4.10, 5.8), and the body calls an EAR a "40% APR" (4.2).
- "checking balance" is a US term, and "/month", "/ year", "~" are used in prose (3.4, 4.1).
- No figure is called an estimate (4.4.1).

### Needs the owner

See "Rulings of 2026-11-14" near the top of this document: several items below are now decided.

- `packages/ui/src/calculators/TaxTrapCalculator/copy.ts:41` "Unverified estimate": fixed wording, unchanged. The tool page subtitle and the catalogue description also say it, so the taper page shows it more than once (guide 4.4.8 says once per surface).
- `packages/ui/src/calculators/Section24Calculator/Section24Calculator.tsx:235`: a tax output with no "Unverified estimate" in the calculator body (R6). Only the page subtitle carries it, so the calculator embedded elsewhere would have none.
- `packages/ui/src/calculators/TaxTrapCalculator/copy.ts:38` and `:52`: the childcare lines state the £100,000 limit beside the person's estimated income. Guide 7.5 allows signposting only, with no eligibility estimate. The rows above change punctuation only; whether the status line stays is an owner call.
- "free" in four sign-up buttons (`DebtSnowballCalculator.tsx:49`, `MortgageVsIsaCalculator.tsx:51`, `Section24Calculator.tsx:48`, `ZeroPercentCliffCalculator.tsx:36`): pricing wording (guide 7.3, 7.4). The proposed label drops it; the owner may want it kept with evidence in the claim register.

## PR 11. One catalogue and tool pages

The slug `uk-100k-tax-trap-calculator` (and the marketing alias for it) is a URL, not copy. It stays as it is; only the visible names change. The same goes for `uk-student-loan-overpay-or-invest-calculator`. No title or meta description in this group still asks "should you".

Unsupported feature claims (E7), checked against the calculator source in `packages/ui/src/calculators`. Each has a corrected row below that describes only what the tool does today:

- Overdraft: the calculator takes a balance and a rate and shows interest for a year and a month. It has no payment input and no payoff. Claims of "payoff" or a falling balance are at `tools.$slug.tsx:249`, `:250` (web) and `ToolsIndexPage.tsx:153`, `:154` (marketing).
- Student loan: the calculator takes a plan, a salary and a balance. It has no overpayment input, no investment comparison and no salary growth. Claims are at `toolsCatalog.ts:63`, `:65`, `tools.$slug.tsx:273`, `:276` (web) and `ToolsIndexPage.tsx:165`, `:166` (marketing).
- Section 24: the calculator takes rent, mortgage interest, other costs and other income. It has no pension input. The pension claim is at `ToolsIndexPage.tsx:184` (marketing only; the web catalogue line 81 does not make it).
- Debt methods: the calculator models one credit card and one loan. "and overdrafts" is at `tools.$slug.tsx:186`.
- "syncs", "monitors", "guaranteed", "Save £…", "dangerous" and "stealth" do not appear in any PR 11 file. They are in the calculator bodies these pages render, with rows in the PR 10 section (mortgage vs ISA lines 148, 291, 337, 340; overdraft lines 73, 125, 128).

The marketing files are being fixed by another session. They are scored and their rows are listed here, each marked "marketing pass session owns this file".

One title and one description per tool, for both apps (D11). Rows below use this set:

| Tool                         | Short title           | Title                                                                                                                                   | Description                                                                                                                                                        |

### Web `/tools` index and catalogue: 5/10

Problems:

- Two visible names say "tax trap" (R8, guide 6.3). The tool is also sixth of seven, and the plan and owner rule list it last.
- "mathematical interest savings against psychological momentum" (guide 6.2; known #12).
- Titles promise things the calculators do not do: "overpay vs invest" and "with and without voluntary overpayments" for a tool with no overpayment or investment input (7.4).
- "nothing you enter is saved" needs checking: the calculators write entered figures to local storage when the sign-up button is used (`persistSeed` defaults to true) (7.4).
- "standard UK rates of up to 39.9% EAR" is an unsourced market claim and an anchor (7.4, 4.4.10).
- Every title differs from the tool page h1 and from marketing (consistency; D11).

### Web tool pages and guides (`routes/tools.$slug.tsx`): 3/10

Problems:

- "Tax trap" appears in a meta title, an h1 and a guide heading, with "60% Marginal Rate" as the hook and "bring adjusted net income back to £100,000" as the aim (R8, R2, guide 6.1, 7.2).
- The mortgage guide names a fund type as the investment ("a global index fund"), and the overdraft FAQ steers towards repaying over saving (R3, R2).
- Meta titles are in Title Case with ampersands; "Stocks & Shares ISA" appears nine times (5.1, 5.4).
- Titles and descriptions claim things the calculators do not model: overdraft "payoff", student loan "overpayment", debts "and overdrafts" (7.4).
- Unsourced market figures stated as fact: "35%–39.9% EAR", "24.9% to 29.9% APR", "up to 10%", "1% to 2.5%" (7.4; each needs a source or "check").
- Jargon and long sentences in the guides: "Prior to", "tax liability", "revert cliff", "statutory cancellation", "e.g.", sentences of 28 to 33 words (3.1, 3.2, 6.1).

Removed by plan: the category badge strings at lines 44, 92, 139, 190, 227, 253, 279; the "Illustration only" note at line 334.

The Section 24 list "Other rules that affect the figures" (lines 157 to 163) meets the rulings-log test: each item states the rule with its costs and conditions, with no "mitigation" or "save tax" framing, and it does not sit under the person's own computed figure. No rewrite; see "Needs the owner".

### Marketing tools index and tool page: 3/10

Problems:

- The card title says "tax trap" (R8, guide 6.3), and the tool is seventh of eight, not last (plan, owner rule).
- The Section 24 description frames pension contributions as a way to "change the taxable bands" and says the rule "impacts higher-rate landlords" (R2, rulings log: no mitigation framing). The calculator has no pension input (7.4).
- Descriptions describe inputs the calculators lack: overdraft "regular payments", student loan "salary growth assumptions" and "paying it down" (7.4).
- "Guidance, not advice." is hand-written into one card description (5.7; see "Needs the owner").
- Title Case in the meta title template and "Income tax" in two badges; a 35-word description; "wealth" and "modeller" (5.1, 3.1, 6.1).
- Titles, descriptions and the h1 differ from the web app's (D11; plan h1 "Free UK money calculators").

Removed by plan: `ToolsIndexPage.tsx:209` "Public tools directory" (eyebrow). The badge strings at 144, 150, 156, 162, 168, 174, 180, 186 go with the category badge on the tool page; if any survive on the index, 162 becomes "Mortgages and investing" and 174 and 180 become "Income Tax" (6.1, 5.1).

### Signed-out footer and shell: 9/10

Problems:

- The blurb lists "budget" calculators, and the catalogue has no signed-out budget tool (7.4).
- The first four footer links take the catalogue's short titles, so "Snowball vs avalanche" and the rest change with the catalogue rows above (D11). Once the income-over-£100,000 tool is listed last it stays out of the footer's first four, as the owner rule asks.

No "live", "syncs" or "monitors" appears in the footer, breadcrumb, tile or grid. Those claims are in the calculator bodies (PR 10) that these pages render.

Estimates for guidance, not advice. | UK debt, mortgage, student loan and tax calculators. ### Needs the owner

See "Rulings of 2026-11-14" near the top of this document: several items below are now decided.

- `apps/web/app/features/signed-out/components/SignedOutFooter/SignedOutFooter.tsx:20` "Estimates for guidance, not advice." and `apps/marketing/src/pages/ToolsIndexPage.tsx:160` "Guidance, not advice.": hand-written notice wording outside the canonical constant (R6, guide 5.7). Not reworded here. The owner decides whether each stays, goes, or comes from the constant.
- `apps/web/app/routes/tools.$slug.tsx:157` to `:163`, "Other rules that affect the figures": allowed under the 2026-11-13 R2 ruling, which is still pending owner approval and is open point 5 for the solicitor (whether public guides may list tax rules that reduce tax at all). The worked example at `:65` shows a pension contribution lowering Income Tax by about £6,000 and falls under the same question.
- `apps/web/app/routes/tools.$slug.tsx:99` "Past performance is not a guide to the future and your capital is at risk." and `:123` "Values can fall as well as rise, and you may get back less than you put in.": risk-warning wording in a regulated style. Left unchanged; the owner and solicitor should confirm Flump wants to carry it and in this form.
- "Unverified estimate" is typed by hand into descriptions (`tools.$slug.tsx:43`, `:138`; `ToolsIndexPage.tsx:172`, `:178`) and the taper calculator shows its own badge, so one page can show it twice (guide 4.4.8: once per surface). The wording is fixed and unchanged here; placement needs a decision.
- `apps/web/app/routes/tools.$slug.tsx:57` and `:58`: the childcare paragraphs state scheme limits and amounts (up to £2,000, £4,000). Guide 7.5 says benefits are signposted only, with no eligibility or amount estimate. These are scheme rules, not estimates for the person, but the owner should confirm that reading.
- "Free" in the index h1 and meta titles ("Free UK money calculators", "Free UK calculator"): pricing wording (guide 7.3, 7.4). Kept as the plan prescribes; it needs its entry in the E7 claim register.
- `apps/web/app/routes/tools._index.tsx:20` "nothing you enter is saved": a privacy claim. The calculators write entered figures to local storage when the sign-up button is used unless `persistSeed` is false, and the web tool wrappers do not set it. The owner should confirm the claim or the behaviour.

## PR 12. Marketing pages

All marketing copy needs owner sign-off before merge (plan decision D15). Rows below are proposals. Sentences about regulatory status, security guarantees, pricing and data handling are not rewritten: they are under "Needs the owner".

### Home (all sections and FAQ): 4/10

Problems:

- Never-use words in visible copy: "baseline", "surplus", "discretionary", "engine", "cashflow", "manual-first" (guide 6.1, 6.2, 3.2).
- "Live" and "demo" on a signed-out page for the sample-data sandbox, which the visitor's session does not make live (guide 6.2 last line, 6.1 "sample data", E7).
- The example preview's figures do not agree with each other: left over is £672 in one place and £840 in another, £756 a month is shown as £195 a week, and "take-home" of £4,200 has tax taken from it. One example figure has no "Example" label (guide 4.4.10, 2.3.4, 7.4).
- "/mo" and "/wk" in prose and captions, ampersands, Title Case labels, an emoji flag and a full stop on the h1 (guide 4.1, 5.1, 5.4).
- "Zero setup hassle" is a minimiser, and "generic budgeting apps" compares instead of describing (guide 2.3.7, 2.2).
- Long sentences in the FAQ (38 and 27 words) and the taper notice (30 words) (guide 3.1).
- Removed by plan: all of `apps/marketing/src/components/McpSpotlightSection/McpSpotlightSection.tsx` leaves the home page (D13, MCP becomes a footer link and one FAQ answer); the hero pill "Built for UK tax rules" goes with the monospace pills; the data-flow diagram in `SecurityPrivacySection.tsx:156-175` goes if the section becomes the short security strip. Rewrites for the diagram are given in case it stays.

### Needs the owner

See "Rulings of 2026-11-14" near the top of this document: several items below are now decided.

- **Sign-off for the whole group.** Every row above is a proposal until the owner approves it (D15). The E7 register (`docs/audit/plans/E7-claims-design.md`) still has an empty "Approved wording (owner)" column for every claim.
- **No-payments guarantee.** "Flump has no feature to make payments or move money." at `apps/marketing/src/components/HomeFAQ.tsx:46`, `SecurityPrivacySection.tsx:206` (and "cannot make payments" at `:167`), `pages/SecurityPage.tsx:81`, `:91`, `:102`, and "Flump MCP tools have no payment feature." at `pages/McpPage/McpPage.tsx:203`. Security guarantee, E7 S4. Evidence exists (the provider interface has no payment method). Needs approved wording and a decision on saying it once per page.
- **Pricing statements that disagree.** `HomeFAQ.tsx:61-62` says "Pricing for Flump has not been announced." in answer to "Is Flump free?". `pages/PricingPage/PricingPage.tsx:74`, `:87` and `pages/ContactPage/ContactPage.tsx:48` say "Flump is a free, invite-only beta. Nothing is charged." `ContactPage.tsx:89` says "Nothing is charged during the beta." Pick one statement (E7 H5, P1).
- **"Free" with a condition.** `PricingPage.tsx:120` "Free", `:124` "Free today", `:149` "A possible future paid plan. Nothing is charged today and it cannot be purchased.", `:167` "Everything in Free", the placeholder price and its label (`PRICING.label`). Guide 7.4 wants the condition in the same sentence as "free". "Browse free calculators" (`HomePage.tsx:207`, `BigMovesSection.tsx:120`) needs confirming as unconditional.
- **Bank connection status.** "Bank connection (planned, not available)" at `PricingPage.tsx:171` against "Bank connection is coming soon" at `HomeFAQ.tsx:50`, `SecurityPrivacySection.tsx:148`, `:174`, `:205`, `SecurityPage.tsx:81`, `:91`, `:111`. D1-b chose "coming soon". "Coming soon" is itself a promise with no date: confirm it still holds.
- **MCP personal-data claim.** `McpPage.tsx:120`, `:136-138`, `:262`, `:277`, `:307` and every catalogue description say the tools read "your saved" figures. E7 A3 recorded that the local CLI's token call returned default data, and said not to claim personal-data access until that was fixed. Two tools say "Not yet available in the local preview" (`:70`, `:76`). Confirm what a preview user gets today before these stay in the present tense.
- **MCP security and data-handling sentences.** "read-only" and "None of them can change your data." (`McpPage.tsx:262`, `:277`, `:307`), "Read-only guardrails" and the `readOnlyHint` sentence (`:200-203`), "Institution names are replaced with generic labels by default." (`:190`, E7 A4), the rate limits "60 tool calls/min, 300 calls/hr" (`:225`), and the token expiry and revoke sentence with "90 days" (`:237`). Each needs evidence in the register. The limits sentence also needs plain words ("runaway autonomous loops", "server spam").
- **"Local CLI (preview): run Flump MCP from source. Setup guide coming soon."** `McpPage.tsx:213-214`, `:258-259` and `McpSpotlightSection.tsx:90`. This is the E7 A1 draft wording, shown three times, in terms a household reader will not know. Needs approved wording.
- **"Unverified example"** at `components/PayRiseTeaser/PayRiseTeaser.tsx:109-110` and "These figures are an unverified example, not advice." at `McpSpotlightSection.tsx:180`. The fixed wording for tax outputs is "Unverified estimate" (guide 7.3, R6). Decide whether a marketing example may vary it.
- **Hand-written disclaimer.** "These are illustrations to help you think it through, not advice." at `components/BigMovesSection/BigMovesSection.tsx:92`. Guide 5.7 says never write a second disclaimer by hand. Decide whether this section takes the canonical notice or none.
- **Vulnerability reporting.** `components/DisclosurePolicySection/DisclosurePolicySection.tsx:18-30` and `ContactPage.tsx:52`: the address is a placeholder that "is not monitored yet" (E7 D4), so a report today goes nowhere. "We do not run a paid bug bounty programme." is a security policy statement. Replace the placeholder before launch.
- **Privacy and complaints routes.** `ContactPage.tsx:114-134` give only an email address. There is no privacy policy page (E7 S7) and no statement of how a complaint is handled. Any sentence added here is data-handling or regulatory wording.
- **Company line in the footer.** `components/Footer/Footer.tsx:33-36` renders placeholder company details (E7 H10, D6, plan known #13). Not copy to rewrite; replace before launch.
- **Comparison with AI models.** `McpPage.tsx:165` says general-purpose language models can get UK tax sums wrong. A rewrite is proposed above, but it is a claim about other products and needs evidence or removal (guide 7.4).

## Shared surfaces A. `packages/ui` (outside calculators) and web files no page PR owns

> **Applied on 2026-11-14 in the shared-surfaces PR.** Rows not applied, and why: a row marked "check:", "Leave current" or "Needs the owner"; a row that needs markup or logic rather than a string (the 5 April day count in `AllowanceProgressPill`, the "{…} calls" plural, removing the "Official MCP SDK" badge, the "Commit to Profile" button in the unused `GlobalBaselineBar`, the `SliderField` accessible name, which 20 page-owned tests select on); `onboarding-seed.ts` names, which are idempotency keys; `ASK_NO_ACCOUNTS` in MCP, because a tool that stages imports exists; and the context pack key `recommendedSacrifice`, which is an engine type that page files read. These go to the final sweep.

Line numbers are the source line the text sits on (checked in the file), which for JSX text is often one line after the inventory's line. `{…}` is an interpolated value.

### `packages/ui` patterns and primitives: 3/10

Most of the package is clean (ScenarioZone, ProjectionNotice, KpiCard, Money, Stepper, ConfirmDialog, InlineAlert and nearly all of `TakeHomeDeltaPanel/copy.ts` need nothing). The score is set by three older patterns: `DebtToWealthChart`, `AllowanceProgressPill` and `GlobalBaselineBar`. `GlobalBaselineBar` is exported but used nowhere in the apps, so deleting it is an alternative to the rows below. `templates/PageTemplates` is Storybook-only and was not audited, but it holds guard hits (see the last two rows).

Problems:

- Advice-shaped wording in `DebtToWealthChart`: it presents moving a repayment "into a compound ISA" as "the real breakthrough" (R2, R3, guide 2.2), and `GlobalBaselineBar` offers "Commit to Profile" from a what-if (R5, guide 5.1 zone table).
- Glossary breaches: "liabilities", "wealth", "baseline", "gross", bare "sacrifice" (guide 6.1, 6.2).
- A countdown badge in the warning style ("{…} days left until 5 April") is urgency about an ordinary allowance (guide 2.3 rule 8, R2).
- Numbers: "Year {…}" as a date, "/mo" on headline figures, a percentage with no "of what", example figures with no "Example" or "Estimate" label (guide 4.1, 4.2, 4.3, 4.4).
- Title Case headings and labels, an ampersand, abbreviations such as "Sal Sac" and "yrs" (guide 5.1, 5.4, 3.2).
- Same concept, different words: "gross pay" in the take-home panel against "salary before tax" in the glossary; "Reset" against the fixed "Reset to saved figures" (guide 6.1, 5.1).

### Imports: 8/10

Problems:

- "Stage", "staged" and "stages" are internal words a household reader will not know. The connection card's own hint already says "prepare" (guide 3.2, and consistency with `McpConnectionCard.tsx:35`).
- The undo dialog's second button is "Keep import"; the guide wants "Cancel" beside a confirm button that repeats the verb (guide 5.9).
- Everything else (errors, empty state, counts, dates) follows the guide.

- "Something went wrong" appears four times, as a badge, a body and two action messages (guide 5.6).
- Negative contractions in errors ("couldn't", "don't", "can't", "hasn't"), where the guide asks for "could not", "do not" (guide 2.1).
- "Please try again" with no cause, and "Trying again..." with three full stops instead of the ellipsis character (guide 5.6, 5.3).
- Same thing, different words: "Skip to content" here against "Skip to main content" in `AppShell`; "Go to dashboard" against the nav's "Home" (consistency, guide 6.1).

### AI connections card: 4/10

Problems:

- Claims with no evidence in view: "Official MCP SDK v1.0", "Setup guide coming soon", and a feature strip that reads as a list of assurances (E7, guide 7.4). See "Needs the owner".
- Developer words throughout: "deterministic", "finance engine", "rate limited", "req/min bucket", "runaway loops", "step-up verification", "client", "context pack", "snapshot", "live agent" (guide 3.2, 6.1, 6.2).
- Title Case on almost every heading, label, button and table header; two exclamation marks; two ampersands; "e.g."; en-US "Anonymize" (guide 5.1, 5.4, 3.2, 3.4).
- Buttons are not verb and object in 1 to 3 words ("+ Generate New AI Connection Token", "Copy Context Pack to Clipboard"), the pending state does not repeat the verb, and success text celebrates ("Copied to Clipboard!") (guide 5.3, 5.10).
- One concept, many words: "AI connection", "connection", "client", "agent", "token"; "generate" and "create"; "revoke" where the guide's word for ending an AI connection is "disconnect" (guide 6.1, 5.9).
- The token is shown once and matters for safety, so its message must be plain and complete, not a shouted heading (guide 5.10).

### Page titles and config: 4/10

Covers `utils/pageConfig.ts`, `public/locales/en/common.json`, `utils/taxTrapCopy.ts`, `services/onboarding-seed.ts`, `utils/demoData.ts`, `hooks/useUserProfile.ts`, `routes/ping.tsx`. Nothing to change in `utils/support.ts`, `components/core/dialogs/TaxDisclaimerButton.tsx`, the import route titles or `apps/marketing/src/utils/site.ts`.

Problems:

- Regulated words in descriptions every signed-in user sees: "High-yield accounts" twice (R3) and "the £100k tax trap" in the nav (R8, guide 6.3, 7.2).
- The page and nav descriptions are feature jargon: "payoff engine", "wealth bridge", "amortisation", "P&L logs", "tax liability", "cash flow surplus", "bank sync", "debt freedom" (guide 6.1, 6.2, 2.2).
- Seeded account and budget names are Title Case and one says "baseline"; these become the person's own account names (guide 5.1, 6.2).
- "£39k" in a budget line name, where prose writes the figure in full (guide 4.1).
- "Log in" and "Log out" in the locale file against "Sign in again" and "Sign out" on the error pages (consistency).
- Two fallback errors start "Failed to" and give no next step (guide 5.6).

### Needs the owner

See "Rulings of 2026-11-14" near the top of this document: several items below are now decided.

- `apps/web/app/features/ai-connections/components/McpConnectionCard/McpConnectionCard.tsx:257` "Official MCP SDK v1.0": a technical claim with no entry seen in the E7 register. Proposed above as a removal; the owner decides.
- `McpConnectionCard.tsx:251` "Preview: MCP tools currently run against sample/baseline data", `:334` "Flump has no feature to make payments", `:311` institution names replaced "by default", and `:35` "It cannot confirm imports or delete anything": feature and security statements. Each needs evidence and an approver under E7 before any rewording ships (the rows above keep their meaning).
- `apps/web/public/locales/en/common.json:51` "Free calculators": a pricing word (guide 7.4, "free" with no condition). Left unchanged; confirm it is in the E7 register.
- `packages/ui/src/patterns/PersonalAllowanceTaperBanner/PersonalAllowanceTaperBanner.tsx:122-124`: the sentence saying funded childcare hours and Tax-Free Childcare "are not available" above £100,000 is an eligibility statement about government support (guide 7.5 says signpost only) and runs to 29 words. Left unchanged; the owner decides whether it stays, and if so it needs splitting in two.
- `packages/ui/src/patterns/TakeHomeDeltaPanel/copy.ts:120` "Unverified estimate. These tax figures have not yet been checked and signed off. Check them with HMRC before you rely on them.": extends the fixed "Unverified estimate" wording with an instruction. Not rewritten; confirm this longer form is the approved one.
- "Net pay" (`TakeHomeDeltaPanel/copy.ts:164`, `GlobalBaselineBar.tsx:65`): glossary 6.1 bans "net pay", but here it is HMRC's name for a pension arrangement, not take-home pay. Proposed "Net pay arrangement"; the glossary needs an explicit exception.
- `packages/ui/src/patterns/GlobalBaselineBar` "Commit to Profile": the button's handler saves what-if values, which R5 forbids whatever the label says. The component is unused; the owner may prefer to delete it.

## Shared surfaces B. MCP tool and prompt text, and finance-engine strings that reach a screen or a model

> **Applied on 2026-11-14 in the shared-surfaces PR.** Rows not applied, and why: a row marked "check:", "Leave current" or "Needs the owner"; a row that needs markup or logic rather than a string (the 5 April day count in `AllowanceProgressPill`, the "{…} calls" plural, removing the "Official MCP SDK" badge, the "Commit to Profile" button in the unused `GlobalBaselineBar`, the `SliderField` accessible name, which 20 page-owned tests select on); `onboarding-seed.ts` names, which are idempotency keys; `ASK_NO_ACCOUNTS` in MCP, because a tool that stages imports exists; and the context pack key `recommendedSacrifice`, which is an engine type that page files read. These go to the final sweep.

Notes for whoever applies these rows:

- Rows marked "(part)" replace only the quoted fragment inside a longer string on that line. Everything else on the line stays.
- `${…}` expressions are copied from the source and must be kept as written.
- Every proposed MCP string was checked against `ADVICE_PATTERNS` (`packages/finance-engine/src/compliance/advice-lexicon.ts`), `PROVIDER_NAMES` and `BANNED_KEY_PATTERN` in `packages/mcp/src/policy/output-policy.ts`. None contains "recommend", "optimal", "maximise", "actionable", "you should", a "best ..." phrase, a provider name, or "move/put/invest/switch" within 40 characters of "ISA", "SIPP", "pension" or "savings account". No result key or tool name is changed.
- Several of these strings are asserted word for word in tests (`read-tools.test.ts`, `query-tools.test.ts`, `simulate.test.ts`, `health-score.test.ts`, `mcp.service.spec.ts`, evals). Update the expectations with the copy.
- There are no MCP prompts in `packages/mcp/src` (the old prompt file was deleted in #637), so nothing to score there.

### MCP read tools: 5/10

Problems:

- Glossary words a model will repeat to the person: "assets", "liabilities", "surplus", "discretionary", "emergency runway", "snapshot", "profile" (6.1, 6.2, 5.14).
- "(s)" plurals in result text: "debt(s)", "account(s)", "page(s)", "record(s)" (5.4).
- Money written as "25 pounds" and band figures with no pound sign (4.1).
- `ASK_NO_ACCOUNTS` asks "Which accounts and balances should I add?" although the server only reads and models, so it implies the assistant can add accounts (7.4), and uses "should" (2.3).
- The same thing has two names: "interest rate" and "APR" for a debt, "stored", "saved" and "recorded" for held figures, "Tax page" capitalised (5.1, consistency).
- Descriptions run to five or more sentences where 5.14 asks for one or two, and month parameters say "YYYY-MM" rather than "in the form 2025-04" (5.14). Only the wording is fixed below, not the length.

### MCP simulate tools: 5/10

Problems:

- An error hint tells the person what to do with their debts: "Simulate again once some debts are cleared or combined" (R2, and "cleared" is on the never list, 6.1).
- "Simulate" and "simulation" in titles, descriptions and notes, where the glossary word is "model" (6.1). The tool names themselves stay (see Needs the owner).
- "surplus", "deficit", "discretionary" and "clear" used for budget and debt figures (6.1, 6.2).
- Money as "20,000 pounds" and "25 pounds" (4.1).
- The net worth tool never says its output is an estimate, and its allocation rule (debt first, then investments) sits in notes as a bare statement instead of a stated assumption (4.4).
- "avalanche" and "snowball" lead, with the plain description in brackets; the glossary puts the plain words first (6.1).

### Finance-engine strings: 2/10

What reaches a reader, checked with grep:

- `formatAiContextPackMarkdown` and the pack JSON (`ai-context-pack.ts`, with `health-score.ts` components, observations and stage headline and subline, and `debt-payoff.ts` text) are returned by `GET /mcp-tokens/context-pack` and copied or downloaded from the AI connections page, to be pasted into a model. User-facing and model-facing.
- `tax-calculator.ts` category names are rendered on the tax page (`TaxRecordsTable`, `TaxIncomeSectionCard`) and in the mobile companion. User-facing.
- `debt-payoff.ts:398`, `:622`, `:743` are rendered by `packages/ui` calculators. User-facing.
- Not user-facing, skipped: `tax-rules.ts` year `description` labels (lines 97 to 596, nothing reads the field); `test-vectors/*`; `uk-rule-sources.ts` and `uk-scheduled-rules.ts` "UNVERIFIED:" notes (rule metadata, only the boolean reaches a screen); `uk-tax-year.ts:60-61` and `:412-417` (thrown developer errors); `tax-calculator.ts:342` (a note stored on a copied record, already plain).
- Already right, no rows: `picture/selectors/*` assumption notes; `disclaimers.ts` (fixed wording).

Problems:

- The context pack grades the person: "Overall Health Score … [Grade: CRITICAL]", "Current Financial Stage", "Priority bills are at risk", "to prevent relapsing into debt" (2.3 rules 1 and 8, R4).
- Advice-shaped and banned wording in health-score component text: "needing immediate Avalanche payoff", "allowance optimisation", "Tax Trap Mitigation", "Caught in the UK 60% tax trap", "fully optimized" (R2, R8, 6.3).
- Flump-set targets stated as the person's: "£1,000 starter buffer", "Target: 3-6 months", "3 months would be £…" (2.3 rule 6).
- Glossary and plain words: "Assets", "Liabilities", "Liquid", "runway", "surplus", "Deficit", "Weighted Cash Yield", "Gross Salary", "high-APR", "Utilized", "clear" (6.1, 6.2, 3.4).
- Title Case, block capitals and ampersands throughout the pack headings and the tax category names; "/mo", "/wk", "£20k", and a per-day figure (5.1, 5.4, 4.1).
- The tax section of the pack carries no notice and no "Unverified estimate", and two figures are stated as fact when they are defaults (4.4, R6; see Needs the owner).
